locality mentioned In the Schedule hereto annexed;

(Acquisition arid Development) Act. 1957 (20 of 1957), the Cenlral Government hereby gives notice of Lis intention to 
prospect for coal therein' 

The plan bearing number C-l (E'JlJl/J JNR/763 -0705 dal ed he 18lh July, 2008 of the area covered by ihis notification 
can be inspected in the office ofthe Western Coalfields Limited (Revenue Department), Coal Estate, Civil Line*, Nagpur- 
440001 (Maharashtra) or atlhe office ofthe Chief General Manager (Exploration Division), Central Mine [fanning and 
Design Institute Gondwana Place. Kanlcc Road, Ranchi or at the office ofthe Coal Controller, l. Council House Sired, 
Kolkata or at the office ofthe District Collector, Yavatmal (Maharashtra). 

Alt persons interested in the lands covered by thisnotificalion shall deliver all maps, charts and other documents 
referred to in sub-section (7) of Section 13 ofthe said Act to the Officer un Special Duty (UR). Western Coalfields Limited.

57J0 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: OCTOEiER 18,2008/ASVINA 2A i fP^i H— Six'. 3(ii)J 

Ministry nO’elroleum & Natural Gas. 

New Delhi, the 15lh Octobber, 2008 

5.0.289 7,— Whereas, by the notification of the Government oi India in the Ministry 
of l’etroktirti and Natural Gas S.O. number 1289 dated 30.05,2^08 issued under sub¬ 
section (I) of section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of 
Cser in land) Act, 1962 (50 of 19(>2). the Central Government declared its intention to 
acquired the right of user in the land m Tehsil : Kalol, District ; I’unchmuhul in the state 
ol Giiiai iii for the transportation of Petroleum Product from Koval i to Katlam in 

the stale of Gujarat a Pipeline should he laid bv the Indian Oil Consolation Limited. 

And vs lie read, the copies of the said Gazette Notification were made available to the 
public on the 16.08,2008. 

And u-heretLS, the Competent Authority in pursuance of sub-section f 1) of section 6 of 
the said Act submitted his report to the Central Government. 

And whereas the Central Government after considering the said mpu-t is satisfied thal the 
right of user in the land speeilied in the Schedule appended to this notification should be 
acquired, 

Now. therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section ! I t of section 6 of the 
said Act. the Central Gov eminent hereby declares that the right of user in the land 
specified in the schedule appended to this notification is hereby acquired for laying the 
pipeline. 

And further, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section t-G of seetion 6 of the 
said Act. the Central Government herebv directs that the right of user in the said lands 
shall instead of vesting in ihe Central Government vest on the date of publication of'this 
declaration in the Indian Oil Corporation Limited free from the tmeumbr uncos.

AWARD 

This is a reference made under Section 10(1 )(d) of 
Industrial Disputes Act. The reference is : 


f< Whether che action of the management of M/e, 
Kerala industrial Polymers Ltd<, Trivandrum in 
denying employment to Shri M + Anandan, Mining 
worker employed in their Clay Mines of 
Sasthavanom* Thiruvananthapuram Dbtt. w^t 
27-8-2000 onward is justified ? If not, to what relief 
the workman concerned is entitled T 

2, Facts of the case in brief are as follows:— 

Shri Anandan was a Mining worker in M/s. Kerala 
Industrial Polymers Limited, Thiruvananthapuram since 
1 8-2-198 6. He was terminated from service w.e.f 27-11 -2000. 
The management at the time of termination of the service 
offered compensation to retrenched workmen which was 
received by 32 employees But the workman in this case 
and another person did not accept it. They raised dispute. 
The case of the workman Sri Aoandan is espoused by the 
union, 

3, According (a the union there is no closure of the 
factory as alleged by the management. The denial of 
employment on the guise of closure of the factory is 
incorrect. The factory continues to operate in a clandestine 
manner. The contention of the management that all 
employees were dismissed from service due to closure of 
the factory is with a motive to deny employment to workers. 
The management has not offered proper retrenchment 
compensation- The workman is entitled to be reinstated 
with back wages from the date of dismissal. 

4, According to the management the workers started 
creating indiscipline, obstructive tactics, strike* threatening 
the officers of the company etc. which brought down the 
production and consequently the company suffered loss 
year after year. In the year 1997 December the company 
was closed down. Thereafter in pursuance to conciliation 
and assurance of union leaders the company was re-opened 
and operation restarted in 1998. But after sometime the 
workers went back in their assurance and resorted to the 
same old method of indiscipline, strike, dis-obedlei>ce h 
gheraos etc. The situation of the company went from bad 
to .worse and came to a complete stand still. Hence the 
company had to resort (o closure of mining operations 
from 26-8-2000 onwards. Before that, service of workmen 
were terminated offering them compensation and notice 
pay. Al! except 2 workers accepted the compensation and 
left the service. Two workers raised the dispute and they 
were referred by ibe Government for adjudication. But 
pending the dispute die other workman < Sri Biju) accepted 
compensation and the reference was settled. The closure 
of the company was inevitable due to non co-operation of 
workers and consequent reduction in production and 
financial loss. But workman was once dismissed from 
service for serious misconduct. But on the assurance of 
good conduct in future he was reinstated H The management 
has not denied employment deliberately. Following closure 
of the factory and di5-continuance of mining operations 
the mining lease of the management company was 
transferred to anolher person. The management foctoiy is 
not continuing its operation of mining. The workman is 
gainfully employed and he is not entitled for any relief.

5. In the light oE' ihe above contentions the only

k the termination legu| ?

the workers were nul co-operating and production hod

Thereafter at the intervention of Labour Cornmissioner and

and seeking Government assistance for revival of the unit. 
But things did not improve and hence Exts. M 3 and M 4 
Closure notice* dated 26-B-2000 were pul up on i*u- 


notice board of the factory as well as Mines. The reasons 
stated En Exts M3 and M 4 are the same as herein before 
mentioned, 

8. S 25-FFA says that when an employer intends to 
close down an undertaking 60 days notice has to be given 
lo the appropriate Governnieiit staling the reasons for the 
intended closure of the undertaking. The proviso to the 
Section says that the provision does not apply to an 
undertaking in which le .vidian 50 workmen are employed, 
So far as the Factory in queMion 15 concerned, there were 
more than 50 workers (Ext. M1). Therefore 60 days' nonce 
was necessary before closing down the factory. No such 
notice is seen issued to the Government by the 
management. The closure notices Exts, Mj and M4 arc 
dated 26'0B-2QG0 and the closure became effective on the 
same day, Then the question U whether a breach of the 
provision of25FFA will make the doure if legal. Want of 
notice lo Go vemmenl will only attract penalty as prodded 
in Section 30-A of ]. D. Act. It is held by (he HoiTble 
Supreme Court in Bom hay u ui o n o f J o urn a I ists V. State of 
B ombay 1964 -1LLJ 3 51 at 3 5 6 that a not ie e under S ec tion 
25F @ is not a condition precedent to valid retrenchment. 
On a parity of reasoning, ihe breach of the provision of 
Section 25-FFA (1) would not make the closure illegal but 
only email the penalty con temp kited under Section 30' A 
of the Act, Hence the com cm Eon of (he union, that lor non 
compliance wiih Sec!Eon 25-FFA the closure becomes illegal, 
cannot stand. 

9. Section 25-EFF requires that the employer shall 
give notice and compensation to the workman retrenched 
who has been in continuous seif vice for not less than one 
year in the undertaking. Proviso to the Section says that 
when the undertaking i:* closed down on account of 
unavoidable circumstances beyond the control of ihe 
employer, compensation to be paid can be limited to average 
pay for three months. The explanation to proviso makes il 
t tear t bat fi nanc ia I loss is not n n u n a vo i dab le c i re nmsian c c 
beyond the con trol of ih e v m p I oy cm o I im h the p ay ni ent o f 
compensation contemplated under Section 25-F of ihe Act 
to average pay fur three months. Kdevnnl portion reads a* 
Id l lows :— 

lt 25-FFF. Compendium lo workmen in case (jf 
closi ng dow n of u nd erta Li o ^s.-( [) Win rc an underta ki rig 
is tlusctl down for any reason what-soever, every workman 
who has been in continuous service for not Im Ilian one 
year i n til at undertaki ug i n i med hi! ely before such elosu re 
shall, subject to the pro visions ofsub-seel ion (2), be entitled 
Lu nolicc and com pen sat km in accordance with 13 k 
provislo ns of Section 25-F, as iTIlie workman hud been 
retrenched: 

Provided ihat where ihe undertaking is closed down 
on account of unavoidable e iron in stances beyond ihe 
control of the employer, ihe compensation to be paid to 
I tic workman under clause (b) oF Section 25-1-2 shall not 
exceed his average puy for three months.

58U 


(Explanation.—An undertaking which is closed down 
by reason merely of— 

(i) financial difficulties (including financial losses); or 
(u) accumulation of undisposed of stocks; or 

(fo) the expiry af the period of the lease or licence granted 
to it; or 

(iv) In case where the undertaking is engaged in mining 
operations, exhaustion of the minerals in the area 
in which operatic are carried on, shall not be 
deemed to be closed down on account of 
unavoidable circumstances beyond the control of 
the employer within the meaning of the proviso to 
this sub-sectionT 

10, As per S-25-F the retrenched workman is entitled 
to one month's notice or pay in lieu of notice and 
compensation equivalent to 15 days average pay forevety 
completed year of continuous service. No notice as per 
Section 25-F is seen given to the workman. By Ext. W l 
12,642 was offered as compensation for retrenchment 
Ext. W5 is wage slip of the workman dated 07-02-2000- The 
gross wage par month was R$ + 3,040 ps r 65. The workman 
was in service as regular Mining worker since 1938. Ext 
W3 is the statement of break In service issued by the 
management to the worker- It shows that he was in service 
since 1988 til! he was terminated bom service in 2000. He is 
entitled to get compensation of 15 days* average pay for 
every completed year of service. That is not seen properly 
calculated and included in the amount of compensation 
offered to him as per Ext. Wl letter. Though the 
compensation amount offered is not adequate It does not 
mean that the employer did not give compensation as per 
Section 25-F- What ts to be done by the employer is to 
calculate compensation in accordance with Section 25-F 
(a) and (b) together with whatever other amounts due to 
him. But for want of proper calculation and payment it 
cannot be said that the retrenchment is illegal It is for the 
management to recalculate die compensation amount and 
pay the worker the difference foiling which, it is for the 
worker to claim the amount under the provisions of 
].D. AcL But for that reason the termination of the service 
cannot be said to be illegal. 

In the result, an award is passed finding that the 
action of the management in denying employment to 
workman Sri Anandan b legal and justified. However he is 
entitled to claim difference in compensation through 
appropriate action under the provision* of the ID- Act, 

The award will come into force one month after its 
publication in the Official Gazette,

<S During trial three of the disputants adduced tbetr 
evidence while Management No. 2 examined one of its 
officers as M.W.-I, besides producing four documents 
marked Ext.-A to D. As Management No. 1 did not

FINDINGS 

ISSUE Noi.lAl 

7. These issues are taken up together Jointly for 
easy disposal. 

It is admittedly all parties that tbc work in q uestion 
was awarded to H. £. C. U by Management No. 2 and In 
turn Management No. I was engaged as subcontractor 
by said H. S. C. L. to undertake the said work. 

ISSUE Nos. 2*3 

8. These Issues being Inter-dependant are taken up 
jointly. 

It is pleaded in the claim statement that in order to 
provide employment to the retrenched workers of a out¬ 
going contractor, several settlements bad been reached on 
behalf Of such contract-workers and the Management 
asking the later to impress upon die subsequent contractor 
to eagage dm retrenched workers of the previous contractor 
as per their need. It is also pleaded in the claim statement 
fry the disputants that the jobs of re-building and capital 
repair of coke oven batteries against which they were 
engaged are not of permanent nature and therefore these 


5813 


jobs are only available for 8 to 9 months in a year and that, 
in view of above nature of Jobs, the .workers engaged by a 
contractor arc terminated on completion of such job. These 
averments of the disputants clearly show that they were 
aware of the fact that their services wore terminable with 
the completion of die jobs against which they were engaged. 
In their evidence three of the disputants (W.W,-1,2 and 3) 
hove deposed that they were engaged by the Management 
No. 1 on the above work sometimes in July 1998. The 
evidence of M, W,-1 examined by the Principal Employer 
(Management No. 2) shows that the contract for the above 
jobs was given as per Ext-A to H.S.CL, and as perExt.-C, 
the letter of intent of H. 3, C.L., the said work was 
undertaken by the Management No. I ire sub-contractor 
ofH.S.C.L,, Ext-D shows that on completion of the work, 
the H.S.C.L. was issued with a job completion certificate 
by Management No.2 indicating that the work was 
completed in all respect cm 10-2-1999 (including one month 
maintenance period). The evidence of the Management 
Witness No, 1 further indicates that after completion of die 
above work the H.3.CX., the Principal Contractor has 
already been paid his dues. 

9. Iltus the above,evidence of both disputants 
and Management No. 2 shows that the disputants were 
engaged for six months from July 1998 on a job which was 
to be completed by KM-1999 (excluding maintenance 
period of one month from 10-1-1999), Therefore, when the 
disputants claim that they were refused employment from 
1-1-1999 it shows that their above termination was the 
resultant outcome of completion of the assigned job and 
therefore under Section 2(oo Xbb) it can not be termed as 
a case of retrenchment so as to attract the provisions of 
Section 25- F of the Industrial Disputes Act It is stated by 
the disputants in their evidence that they were teiminated 
from 31-12-1998 as they refused to change iheir Union, but 
such a stand does not Inspire any confidence as the 
disputants could not have continued beyond December 
1998 when the work against which they were engaged was 
seemlnglynot available after December 1998. 

10. It is forther claimed by the disputants that they 
were refused employment without any notice or 
compensation with effect from 1-1-1999. In this regard it 
would be suffice to repeat here that the engagement of a 
winker against a fixed tenure job is liable to come to a close 
on completion of such work and for this no advance notice 
is required to be given before termination. However (he 
evidence of W.W.-l shows that in fret the Management 
No.I had displayed a termination notice in the TimeOfRcc 
with a request to the disputants to collect their dues. But 
the disputants had refliaed to collect the same on protest, 
White advancing an explanation for not accepting their 
dues the witness says that they had refused to receive as 
because they were not communicated individually the 
manner of calculation end on which count their dues were 
calculated, But such of the explanation can not be accepted, 
like their other plea that they have been terminated far their

Union activities, as because their main intention behind 
raising the dispute, as gathered from their c [aim statement, 
being Just prevent workers of another Unions being 
eopttedby Management No, 1. 

11, Thus in view of the above discussion I find 
that the termination of the workmen is not on account of 
(heir Union activities but it was the resultant outcome of 
the session of the work against which they were engaged. 

12. The reference is answered accordingly experts 
against Management No.l and on contest against 
Management No. 2 with no relief to the disputants.

7. Second Party filed written arguments at Ex-30 
whereas first party at Ex-21. 

8. Here charge against second party workman was 
Of absenteeism. It is pertinent to note that, in the cross- 
examlnatkin he admits abaentnism. Even copies of enquiry 
proceedings filed at Ex-13 by the management and more 
precisely, page 19 of It reveals that, while answering to 
question no. 4, this second party has stated that "my

and four occasions was due to awn sickness and rest for

has not disputed the period of absence l.e. 371 days during 
26-10-1996 to 03-03-1998. Charge of misconduct was leveled 
against second party which covers absenteeism and when 
that charge was accepted fay second party workman, in my 
considered view, question arise why detailed enquiry is 
required? At the most explanation has to be SOutht for 
remaining absent without permission. He stetet that 
medical certificate was not considered by Inquiry Officer. 
It is pertinent to note that, no such medical certificate is 
brought on record by the second party to consider at least 
before this Court to justify his absenteeism. The al legation 
is that he has habit to remain absent and to disprove it 
burden Iks on second party to show that, he had reason to 
remain absent. But here though second party workman 
admits absenteeism, appeared before Inquiry Officer, record 
and proceeding re van I j that, he has not filed those

Of his mother? Besides, this is not an isolated incident for 
which he was punished. Even previously he was punished 
by stopping one increment end that is not disputed by 
him* So when there is no evidence of eny type of second 
parly about absenteeism, question of ignoring it by the

absenteeism and when he has not justified it, in my 
considered view, findings given by Inquiry Officer on such 
Mid absenteeism cannot be taken lightly and ignored just 
to help the second party only because he belongs to weaker 
section. No doubt, employer and employee must get equal 
oportunity. No doubt employee must get protection. No 
doubt without following due process of law the employer 
cannot take any action. At the same time it is expected tht r 
employee also should act properly. He should follow the 
procedure. He should Follow the guidelines. He should 
take proper precaution by not remaining absent. Hem, it is 
pertinent to note tht not a single incident is mentioned of 
evidence or any effort made by him which was ignored by 
first party. The allegation of first party is that, without 
intimation and permission he has habit to remain absent 
and that is why it is treated misconduct. In the inquiry he 
accept the charge, and when he accepted charge and 
pleaded guilty of charge then, only question remains of 
justification of that. Here opportunity is not utilised by 
second party to justify his absenteeism by giving evidence 
of any type* Even he has not produced any evidence before 
this Tribunal to observe findings of Inquiry Officer perverse. 
When there was no evidence before Inquiry Officer how 
second party can say that, it was ignored by Inquiry 
Officer in observing that there was no justification for 
absenteeism? 

9. So, if we consider ail these coupled by case 
madeout by both, I conclude that, enquiry was fair and 
proper and findings not perverse. Accordingly, 1 answer 
above issues and pass the following order ; 

ORDER 

1, Enquiry is fair and proper, 

1 Findings not perverse 

3. Both parties to appear on quantum of 
punishment.

AWARD 

The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour* 
in exercise of Powers conferred by Clause (d) of sub-section 
(l) and sub-section 2(A) of Section 10 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947(14 of 1947) have referred the following 
dispute for adjudication vide their Order No. L*290l 1/45/ 
2001 [IR(M)], dated 11-06-2001. 







>wiwb: yuqut a, awsraifrff 26, 1930 


5817 


"Whether the demand of the Keonjhar Mines 
MaxdOor Uni cm At/Po, Gurudo, Dirt. Keotylur, to 
regiiarire Sort. Madhabi Pradhon in the Cadre Post 
of Creche Aya from her date of appointment i.e, 
23-1-1990 and payment of differential wages from 
Picker to Creche Aya by the Management of Orissa 
Mining Corporation Ltd., Barbil is justified? 
If >0 what relief the workman is entitled fbt"? 

2. From the claim statement filed by die Union it 
appears that the disputant-\wrkman Stnt Madhabi Pradhaa 
was appointed as 0 Picker on daily rated basis in the 
S.OJB.K. mines of the Management-Company on23-1-1990, 
As a Picker she would have worked under sun and tain to 
separate different grades of ore. Instead, she was attached 
toaCtecheofTtakurani Mines where one Hafiz* Khatun 
was working aa a Creche Aya. Alter closure of (be aid 
mine the was transferred to another mine celled Siljoda 
Mine and attached to the Creche of that mine In August 
1998, where she is itiUconifouing. It is alleged by the Union 
that as an outcome of unfair Labour practice the 
Management potted the disputant in the Creche of 
Thakurani Mines to discharge die duties of a Creche Aya. 
After closure of die said mine she was again attached to 
the Creche of Sityoda Mine to render the duties of a 
regular Creche Aya, Even though she satisfactorily 
managed the post Of Creche, Aya at Thakurani Mines and 
later in Siljoda Mines she was not paid the salaiy of that 
post. Under the shove bade ground the Union raised an 
Industrial Disputes before the Asstt Labour Commissioner 
(Central) claming his differential pay as also for her 
regularization against a vacant cadre poet of Creche Aya. 
According to the Union one Hafizs Khatun who had 
Initially joined as aMudeor was subsequently regularized 
as a Creche Aya as per the decision in O.J.C. 1994/92 of 
Hou’ble High Court and therefore the disputant Should be 
extended with similar benefits. 

3. The Management on the other hand contends that 
die disputant workman was appointed ss a Picker on com¬ 
passionate ground after her mother took V.R.C. in 1990. 
The job of a Picker being to separate different grades of ore 
under sun and rein, the workman requested the Manage¬ 
ment for her adjustment in some other job end accordingly 
sho was attached to the Creche of Thakurani Mine with 
effect from 23-1-1990 to assist the Creche Aya, Haftea 
Khatun and the nurse. Under the rules, during the 
absence period of Creche Aya the nunc is to place requi¬ 
sitions for supply of different articles. The concerned 
Creche Ayn Hafizs Khatun (W.W.-l) beingan illiterate fody 
the workman had requisitioned for certain articles during 
die absence of the nurse and therefore the said requisi¬ 
tions shall not be made the sole basis to say that the work- 
nun was discharging the dudes of a Creche Aya till she 
was transferred to the Creche of Siljoda Mine in August 
1998. According to the Management on the closure of 
Thakurani Mine all those who were working there were 
transferred to other mines but in view of the pendency of 


the conciliation proceeding before Asstt. Labour Commis¬ 
sioner (Central) the workman was again attached to the 
Creche of Siljoda Mine to assist the Creche Aya and the 
nurse and therefore she is neither entitled to claim the 
safety of > Creche Aya nor eligible to be regularized against, 
that cadre post As regards the regularization of Haflza 
Khatun in the post of Creche Aya it is further extended 
by the Management that the said employee was earlier 
working as a Mszdoor under Sirqljuddin & Co. and after its 
merger with the Management the said Khatun and many 
others filed OJC. 1994/93 before the High Court of Orissa 
claiming parity of treatment in respect of their pay and 
service conditions with similarly placed workman in the 
regular establishment. As per the direction given by the 
Hon 'ble Court the wid Hafizs Khatun and few others were 
given fitment against appropriate posts In the regular 
service of the ManagemenfrCompany. The disputant not 
being a party to that proceeding and her case nbt being 
comparable with the case oFHafizu Khatun, she can not be 
regularized by bypassing the Recruitment Rules. 

4. On the pleadings of the parties the following 
issues were framed. 

ISSUES 

1. Whether the dispute comes under the Industrial 
Dispute* Act? 

2. Whether Smt. Madhabi Pradhan was performing 
the duty of Creche Aya from the date of appointment i.e. 
234-1990? 

3. Whether she is entitled to be regularized as Creche 

Aya? 

4. Whether the demand of payment of differential 
wages from Picker to Creche Aya by the Management of 
Orfesa Mining Corporation Ltd., Barbil is justified?* 

I 

5. To what relief the workman is entitled? 

5. Two witnesses from the side of the Union and 
equal number of witnesses from the side of the Manage¬ 
ment have even examined. From the side of the Union 5 
documents have been marked as Ext,-1 to 5 while no docu¬ 
ment has been produced by the Management. 

FINDINGS 

ISSUE No. 1 

6, When admittedly the disputant was appointed as 
a Picker and as she still continues till date under the same 
design st ion, there is no question of holdingthat herd aims 
do not cotne under the definition of Industrial Disputes. 
In the written statement the Management no doubt alleged 
that the Union in question has got no locus standi to raise 
the dispute but in the obwfflfi* of evidence to that effect it 
cannot be raid so. Hence this issue is answered 
affirmatively. 







THE GAZETTE OF [ND1A: OCTOBER 18,2008/ASVlNA 26 ,1930 


5818 


ISSUE NOS,2,3.4 & 5 

7. These issues being inter-1 inked are taken up 
together. 

It is not under dispute that the workman* though 
was appointed as a Picker, was attached to the crechc of 
Thakuranl Mines from 23-1-1990, 

It is also not disputed that on the closure of the 
Thakurani Mine she was transferred to a creche of another 
mine called Siljoda Mine from 1-8* 1998. Therefore, looking 
at the claim of the Union it is only to be seen whether by 
attaching the disputant to different creches [he 
Management had ever wanted to extract from her the duties 
of a Creche Aya and whether she is entitled to be regularized 
against such post* 

8. The evidence of the Management shows that 
creches arc of different grade and therefore they are being 
managed differently. 

9. It is no record that by the time the workman was 
attached to the creche of the Th&kurani Mine one Hafiza 
Khatun (W.W.-l) was the Creche Aya. This witness (W. 
W F -1) says that prior to the joining of the disputant she 
used to take the assistance of the nurse attached to the 
creche to^ write out her leave application and other 
correspondence as she was illiterate and unable to do 
herself Coming to the duties performed by her and the 
disputant she deposed that as a Creche Aya she used to 
take all cares of the children by dreasing their beds, 
Supplying fend materials and putting them in bed whenever 
required. She also used to cook food for the children and 
all these she used to do as a matter of her duty. As regards 
the duty performed by the disputant she said that she 
being illiterate the disputant used to take the attendances 
of (he children and assist her in looking after the children. 
According to her* on the retirement of the creche nurse she 
faced various difficulties for which the disputant was 
provided to her and that after joining of the disputant she 
used to make all official correspondence through her. Hut 
while narrating all these she did not whisper that like her 
the disputant was also cooking food for the children 
preparing their bed or supplying food to the children. Thus 
from an over all assessment of her evidence it embargoes 
that the disputant was posted there to assist the Creche 
Aya and in that process she used to place requisitions 
occasionally to the authority for supply of materials as 
evident from Ext.-1 to 4 h the in charge Creche Aya [ W. W.- 
1) being admittedly an illiterate lady. During cross 
examination the disputant herself has admitted that she 
was helping the Aya (W, w.-l J during absence of the nurse 
in placing the requisitions on behalf of Aya, the later being 
illiterate. Therefore, in these circumstances. It is hard to 
believe that the disputant was discharging the duties of an 
Aya during her tenure in the creche ofThakurani Mine, As 
I find except placing requisitions during the absence period 


[Past II— Sem.'. 3(Li)J 


of the nurse she was doing no other work which an Aya is 
required to do. Therefore basing on such requisitions Ext - 
1 to 5 it would be wrong to hold that she was rendering the 
duties of an Aya In the Thskuani Mine creche. 

10. As regards the duties performed by ihc disputant 
in the Siljode creche io which she was transferred in 1998 
vide Ext,-j* she herself has admitted during cross- 
examination that* in Siljoda creche she was simply assisting 
the creche Aya but not issuing any requisitions or doing 
any pari of the duties of an Aya. 

IT Thus in the above circumstanced there appears 
no justification fbr the Union to claim regularization of the 
disputant nor for her differential pay, 

12, ll is no doubt on record that Hafize Khatun 
(WhW,-)) joined the Management as a Mazdoor but 
subsequently without any test or recruitment process she 
was regularized subsequently as Creche Aya. But from the 
pleadings and the judgement in G.J.C.. 1994/93 which i* 
available on record it appears that, the said Khatun and 
many others had joined [he Management as ex-employ ecs 
of a merger company known as Sirajjuddin &Co> Ltd, and 
on their preferring the above OJ.C. the Management had 
regularized the said W. W.-l and others os per the direction 
of ihe Hon'ble Court and as such the disputant being 
outside the purview of the said findings, her case can not 
be weighed equal; with that of Hafiza Khatun. 

13. In view of the above discussions 1 find no merit 
In the claim of the Union and as such the reference is 
answered in negation.

AWARD PART-1 

The reference 1* sent to this Tribunal by the Under 
Secretary of Central Government, the Government of India, 


lg,2PW*nft*424 t lPM 3819 

Ministty of Labour by its OderNo. L-30012/W2QQ2/IR (M) 
dated I3lh November, 2002 in exercise of the power* 
conferred by clause (d) of sub-section (1) and sub-section 
[2 A) of Section 10 of the Indus trial.Disputes Act, 1947 to 
decide: 

H Whether the claim of the action of the management 
of Hindustan Petroleum Corporation Ltd,, Mahid 
Refinery, Mumbai in terminating the services of Shrl 
M. J. Rathod, BuB( Operator, w.e.f. I8-S-2001 is legal 
and justified? If not, what relief the workman 
concerned is entitled to? 

2. To support the subject-matter In the reference the 
statement of claim is filed by the 2nd Party at exhibit 7 
stating that, he was employed by the lit Parr/ as a general 
workman with effect from 1st November, 1977 and was

6. Enquiry proceedings are filed at Exhibit 17. It 
reveals that 2nd Party did not participate in the enquiry. 
Even 1st Party has not led evidence by examining Enquiry 
Officer to show that, enquiry was fair and proper and it was 
conducted by following principles of natural justice. Again 
for conducting ‘de-novo' enquiry the reason given by the 
1st Party is that 2nd Party purposely remained absent. But 
that is not proved by leading any evidence. In the absence 
of the evidence from 1st Party and looking to the evidence 
led by the 2nd Party which is not refuted by leading other 
evidence by examining the Enquiry Officer or any other 
witnesses, 1 conclude that, enquiry is not fair and 
proper. 

7- If the enquiry is not fair and proper the finding 
given by the Enquiry Officer which is not on the evidence 
of the 2nd Party cannot be treated as finding on merits. So 
1 answer this Issue to that effect and conclude that the 
enquiry is not fair and proper and finding per verse. Hence, 
the order: 

ORDER 

1 conclude that the enquiry is not fair and proper 
with its finding perverse; vis-a-vis 1 st Party to justify its 
action of termination.

AWARD 

The Government oflndia, Ministry of Labour by its 
Order No. L-30012/13/2006/1R (M) dated 14-7-2006 in 
exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub¬ 
section (1) and sub-section 2(A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 have referred the following 
dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication: 

“Whether the action of (he management of BPCL. 
Mumbai in terminating the services of Sh. Sanjeev 
Desai Emp. No. 37957. by way of dismissal from the 
service of BPCL wt.f, 5-5-2005 is proper, justified 
and proportionate? If not, then what relief the 
workman is entitled to?” 

2. By notice Ex-4, it appears served on second party 
workman but he remained absent. Even lateron. notice Ex- 
7 sent on same address of the workmen returned back by 
postal authorities with remark not claimed. So ihe reference 
is disposed of for want of prosecution. Hence the order: 

ORDER 

Reference is disposed of for want of prosecution.

Government of India vide Notification No. L-42012/ 
55/90-IR (DU) + dated 27-11-90 referred the following 
Industrial dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication: 

* Whether the action of the management of Bhakra 
Beas Management Board, rep. Through the Chief Engineer, 
Bhakra D am, Nangai T ownship in terminating the services 
of Shri Kristian Ram regulation Belder/Daryai-man w.eT 
30-7-33 is justified? If not* to what othenreliefthe workman 
is entitled to and with what effect? 1 

As per the averment made by the workman in his 
statement of claims, he has been in employment with the 
B.B.M.B. since 1973 in all the rainy seasons as Daryai man/ 
regulation Beldar, as unskilled mqjdocron work charged 
basis. He was engaged on rainy seasons only and was 
employed till 29-7-38< Due to the death of his relative, he 
had to proceed to nearby Shtmla about 290 kilometers from 
the site of work. He presented unsppUcationtohislncb^Tfee 
for two days for 30-7-88 and 31-7-33 and reported for duty 
as usual on l*ft-88. He was not allowed to join duty and 
was put on road. For the said cause another case is also 
pending adjudication in reference No. 174190 and in the 
present reference, the workman has claimed re-employment 
in the rainy season of 1939 and so on which is denied to 


the workman. The denial by management to provide rainy 
season work tn 1989 onward is arbitrary and unjust. On the . 
basis of the above averment, the workman has prayed for 
an order of re-employment for the rainy seasons 1989-90 
and so on with back wages. 

It is not disputed by the management of respondent 
that from 1975 to 1987 the workman was employed regularly 
during the rainy season after having been sponsored by 
the employment exchange as per the terms and conditions 
of the appointment. The services of the petitioner came to 
an end after the expiry of each specified period, Upto 

29- 7-38 the petitioner was engaged with the management

The workman ts already gainfully employed working as a

the management of 33MB that he was working as 
contractor and simultaneously employed in the B3MB as 
Duryai-man, 

En his rejoinder the petitioner challenges the 
averments of management of BBMB made in written 
statement. 

Both of the parties were afforded the opportunity to

of BBMB regarding the extraction of sand* bajrl etc. All the 
appointment letters from 1975-1938 are also on record 
containing the terms and conditions of appointment. 

I have heard learned counsels for the parties and 
pursued the entire materials on record. 

Undoubtedly > the workman was working from 1975 
to 1987, regularly with the management of BBMB for rainy 
seasons as Daryai-man/regulation Beldar as unskilled 
majdoor. He was- also provided the work for 1988 and he 
reported to his duty on 2-7-88. The dispute is ihat the 
workman absented for two days he. 30-7-88 and 3 1 -7-88. 
As per the statement of workman* he had to proceed to 
Shimla which is about two hundred kilometers from the 
place or his duty on account of the death of his relative, 
thereafter, he fall ill and was admitted to hospital on 

31- 7-88 and was discharged on 1*8-88. He has stated that 
an application was moved for two days. On the other hand 
the management hasdenied that any application for leave 
was moved by the workman. He absented from duty 
without information and at his place considering the 
exigency of work another person was engaged. 

There are two questions to be answered by this 
TribunaMnthis reference:-

AWARD

Government of India* videNotificationNo. L-4201 2/ 
181/90/IR (DU) dated 13-6-91 referred the following 
Industrial Dispute for judicial adjudication: 


“Whether the action of the Chief Engineer, Bhakra 
Beas Management Board, Nangal Township, in 
term mating the services of Shri Ram Pal, S/o Sh. 
Sandhya Lai, Mazdoor w.e.f^ 1-9-89 is justified? If 
not* what relief the concerned workman is entitled to 
and from what date T' 

As per the averments made by the workman in the 
statement of claim, he was employed in the Nangal Division 
w.e.f 24-7-89, as unskilled Mazdoor and was continuously 
employed till 6-8-89. While on duty, he met with an accident 
on 6-8-89 and suffered an injuty in his left eye. He became 
unconscious and was taken to B,B.M.B. Hospital where he 
was given the First-Aid treatment and was referred to PGl, 
Chandigarh, where he remained admitted from 7-8-87 to 
21-8-89* He was advised to visit PGr on 28-9-89 and was 
fmaEly discharged on 31-8-89, He reported for duty on 
1-9^89, but was not allowed to join duty by the JE and EDO 
in charge. He was refused to join the duty on account of 
being a daily waged employee and'the daily waged 
employee has no claim for the re-employment. The 
management has been recruiting the new hands whereas, 
he was denied the work. Accordingly, he has prayed for 
reinstatement in the services with full back wages with 
cost and damages. 

The management of B.B.M.B. admitted the fact of 
accident on duty. It is denied that after accident, no 
information was given to the management but it is admitted 
the previously, he was treated in the hospital of BJLM-Bh

on 1-9-89 but for the first time, he reported the matter to

was obligatory on his part to inform the JE/5DO about the 
accident imrnmedially on the occurrence of the accident 
which was not dtme r He was a daily wager appointed for 
the monsoon seasons and as the season was over uo 
question for taking him to rolls arise. He was provided the 
full compensation under the Workman Compensation Act, 
admissible to him on account of his accident on duty. 

Both of the parties were afforded the opportunity for 
adducing evidence. In his cross-examination, the workman 
has just repeated the contents of the statement of claim. 
He has admitted that he has got compensation of 
R$. 13,885.87011 account of his accident on duty.

prevented to work with the management on account of the 
accident While he was on duty and this situation was out 
of his control. He should be provided the full back wages 
and the seniority in the service. Learned Legal Advisor for 
the management of has admitted that whenever 

the management will engage any person for the work, the 
workman was carried on, he will be given priority. He has 
also argued that seniority benefit can be given subject to

AWARD 

Passed mi 10-9-2008

wiwn; aregm i s, aoos/anftgq 26 , imo 


5839 


4$ he has become surplus and there was no sufficient work 
for (he 2 part time Sweepers in the branch. 

Beth of the parties were afforded the opportunity for 
adducing evidence. Sh. Mangu Ram vide his affidavit stated 
that the act of providing] ob on 27-8' 1996 was just to escape 
from the legal consequences on account of his illegal 
termination on 15-6-1995, which amounts to unlawful labour 
practices. On the point of unlawful labour practices, he 
was not cross-examined at all. In his cross-examination, 
only one question was asked by the learned representative 
of the management of bank that affidavit of workman is 
wrong to which die workman denied. 

The main question for determination before this 
Tribunal is whether the retrenchment compensation given 
by the bank under the circumstances prevailing in this 
reference is justified? 

For the purpose of answering this reference that 
whether action of the management in terminating the 
services of the workman for 15-6-1995 is justified? The 
answer has been put into the month of this Tribunal by 
way of the cond uct of the management by offering him the 
re-employment. Thus, the conduct by offering re¬ 
employment itself shows that the termination of the 
workman from 15-6-1995 was not legal and justified. The 
Management of (he bank, on the very day, the workman 
joined the bank on re-employment gave him one month 
notice and after retrenchment compensation his services 
were terminated. Thus, act of management certainly 
amounts to unlawful labour practices because it was licit 
done with intention to provide the job to the workman but 
to escape from the legal liability which the management be 
directed by this Tribunal to undertake under the provision 
_ of Industrial Disputes Act. Thus, the retrenchment 
compensation has no legs to stand under these 
circumstances In which it was paid in the present case or 
account of being unlawful labour practices. The termination 
of (he workman remains unlawful and not justified. 

Now the Tribunal has to decide as to what relief the 
workman is entitled? Under such circumstances the two 
possible relief are possible, 

1. Direction by the Tribunal to provide the job of the 
workman on the same terms and conditions or 
which he was retrenched, and 

2 A reasonable compensation to be awarded to the 
workman. 

In this case, it has come to the notice of this Tribunal 
that 2 permanent and one part-time Sweeper are already 
working with the bank and there is no sufficient work for 
the second part-time Sweeper. So in such type of cases a 
reasonable compensation may be an appropriate remedy. 
Thus Tribunal has got the powers to provdc (he reasonable 
compensation in appropriate cases as held by Hon‘ble the 
Apex Court in the following cases;— 


I. Jaipur Development Authority v$. Rem and 
another, 2006(111 \ FLR.1178, SC, 

1 Madhya Pradesh Administration vs. Tribunal, 2007 
(113) FLR886, Supreme Court 3, 

3. Uttaranchal Forest Development Corporation vs. 
M.C. Joshi 2007 (113) FLR 191 Supreme Court. 

All the above mentioned case laws have been 
discussed by Hon'ble the Apex Court in Sita Ram and 
Another vs. Motilal Nehru Farmers Training Institute, 
2008(117) FLR 1191 in which the Hon’bie Apex Court has 
also awarded the compensation instead of reinstatement 
of the workman into the service. 

Thus, considering all the facts and circumstances of 
the case and also considering that some amount of 
retrenchment compensation has been given to the workman 
which he has not received but was transferred to his saving 
bank account, ( am of the view that Rs. 25,000 will be an 
appropriate amount of compensation to be given to the 
workman. Accordingly, the management of the bank is 
directed to deposit Rs. 25000 in the Tribunal as the 
compensation to be given to Sh. Mangu Ram, the workman. 
If the management of die bank fails to deposit the amount 
within one month from the date of (he publication of the 
Award, the workman will be entitled for the interest at the 
rate of 7% per annum from the date of the passing of this 
award till the final payment. ' : 

Accordingly, the reference is answered. Central 
Government be informed. File be consigned. 

G. K. SHAR.MA, [’residing Officer

Ely Adv. Stl V.V. Siddarrhan. 

This case coming up for hearing on 26-G-200S n ibis 
Tribunal-cum-Fa hour Court on 30-6-200S passed the 
following. 

AWARD 

Th is is re fere n ee a i nude an de r S ec lion 1 G( L)(d )of 
[ndustriil Disputes Act. The reference is :— 

Ll Whether the mi ion of "the management oEThe Central 
Bank of India in importing punishment of compulsory 
retirenn ent vv i; ii -j li pemnn u a t ion benofi I s o fS hri P .M. 



[HRII-TSFg3(ti)] 


VRn^7T5PT? r IB, 2008/^wft^ 26,1930 


Umnidiekkan, Teller ofKalanjoor Branch of the Bank 

Mr.e.f. 5-8-2002 is justified ? If not, what relief 

Sh. Unnichekkan is entitled to? 

2 . The facts of the case in brief ate as follows:—. 
Sri P.M, Unukliekkanwas a Teller ofKalanjoor Branch of 
Central Bank of India, On 12*4-2001 he was given a memo 
alleging that be had withdrawn an amount of Rs, 34,000 
from the S. B. Accounts of 4 customers on different dales 
without the knowledge of customers by forging their 
signatures on different dates and without entering the 
withdrawal in their pass books, A charge sheet was issued 
to him which was followed by domestic enquiry. In the 
enquiry he was found guilty of the charge of fraudulent 
withdrawal of money from customer s’account and 
misappropriating it. There was another charge regarding 
making false statements in documents connected with his 
employment. That charge was found not proved, 

3. According to the workman he had not done the 
alleged acts- But he had borrowed some money from 
customers for the purpose of the treatment of his wife. He 
was not able to repay the amounts within time. That could 
be reason why the customers complained. He would 
challengd die findings of Enquiry Officer as well as the 
verdict of the disciplinary authority and appellate authority. 
The disciplinary authority deviated from the findings of 
the Enquiry Officer. But the workman was not heard by the 
disciplinaiy authority before recording a different finding. 
There h no evidence to substantiate the te&e of the 
management fl^dingfriauddeil withdrawal A preliminary 
investigation was conducted by an officer of the bank. 
However it was not produced in the enquiry. Had it been 
produced it would have brought out the truth. At any rale 
the punishment imposed is illegal and disporoportionate. 
The workman is entitled to be reinstated, 

4. According the management there is clinching 
evidence in the enquiry to prove that the workman ha* 
committed the misconduct The workman did not enter the 
withdrawal in the pass book of the customers with the 
intention of misappropriating money, The Disciplinary 
Authority as well as the Appellate Authority have properly 
analysedjthe evidence before recording their conclusions. 
The preliminary investigation report was not relied on by 
the management and hence Its non-grixluction cannot 
affect the enquiry. Th£ punishment was imposed in 
proportion to the gravity of the misconduct. The story of 
borrowing money from customers for the treatment of wife 
of the workman is not substantiated by the workman and it 
is an unbelievable story. The Enquiry Officer conducted 
the enquiry fully complying with principles of natural justice 
and the workman was given full opportunity to defend. 
There is no ground for interfering with the findings or 
punishment. 

5h The points that arise for consideration are:— 
t. Whether the findings are sustainable ? 

2 . Is the punishment proportionate ? 

The evidence consists of the Enquiry File Ext, Ml 
alone on the side of Management. 

d. Point No, I:— In the enquiry 5 witnesses were 
examined on the side of the management and they are MWl, 


5&41 


1 3 5 P 9 and 10. The remaining witnesses in the list were not 
examined. Exts* ME-1 to ME-S7 documents were also 
marked. On defence side workman was examined as DW I 
and documents Ext s, DE-1 to 5 were marked. 

7. The main argument of the learned counsel for [he 
union is that the Disciplinary Authority had deviated from 
the findings of the Enquiry Officer with regard to charge 
No, 1 without hearing the workman. This vitiates the 
enquiiy. What prompted the learned counsel for the union 
to submit so is because of the observation of the Enquiry 
Officer that among the management witnesses only MW9 
had identified the signatures in the disputed withdrawal 
forms as that of the workman. But the same witness failed 
to identify the signature of workman in another withdrawal 
slip. Hence the enquiry officer did not find (he witness 
reliable. Yet the workman was found guilty. Relying on the 
same set of evidence the Disciplinary Authority found the 
workman guilty. It is self evident from the submission that 
the Disciplinary Authority has not made any deviation 
from the findings of the Enquiry Officer, ft is to be noted 
that apart from MW9 h there are other witnesses and a number 
of documents, besides circumstances to prove the 
charge The finding is not based on the testimony oFMW9. 
The other objection that investigation report is not 
produced, cannot affect the enquiry as the management 
has not relied on that report. 

£. The complaints weit not examined as they were 
not interested to appear in the enquiry as they had already 
received the money. Charge No,2 was found not proved 
and it was accepted by the Disciplinary Authority. The 
finding regarding charge No, 1 was confirmed by the 
Disci iponary Authority. Thus there is no deviation from 
the findings of Enquiry Officer. 

9, The next contention is that iti view of the 
observation of the Enquiry Officer lhat none of the 
witnesses have identified the signature in the withdrawal 
slips as that of the workman, cuts at the very root of the 
case of the management and hence the entire findings 
regarding charge No, 1 should crumble down, The 
argument appears to be sound at first blush, but there is 
something beneath the gloss. The case of the management 
is that the workman had withdrawn amounts from the SB 
Accounts of 4 account holders on various occasions to 
the tune of Rs, 3^000. The account holders are 
SmtPonnatmrtaGopl, from whose account Rs. 19,000 was. 
withdrawn on 12 different occasions. From the account of 
Smt. Ponnamma S. Nair Rs. 2,000 was withdrawn. From the 
account of Smt, Pankajakshy Amma and Shri P, Narayanan 
Nair an amount of Rs. 2,000 was withdrawn. From ihc 
account of Smt. Shceja Beevi Rs. 8,000 was withdrawn on 6 
occasions. From che account of Smt, P L G, Ramadevi and 
SathyapaJan Nair an amount of Rs. 3,000 was withdrawn 
on 2 different occasions. Thus withdrawals were made 
without enlering them in the pass books of the account 
holders. However they are accounted in the ledger fofiert 
and Teller Daily Cash Payment Register. The withdrawal 
slips contain fordged signatures of customers. This was 
done by the workman Withdrawal slips are ME9 to 20,23, 
2S and 32 to 36, 40 and 41. The withdrawal slips are 
apparent^ signed by the account holders. But according

This industrial dispute has b«n raised by the wife 
of deceased worker Ishwar Lat Kuril through

and admitted the contents of the settlement.

Kuril

wilhin 30 days from the date of settlement. 

In view-of the aforesaid selllemcnr and stated 
position by both the parties it is no longer necessaiy to 
go into the merit of the claim. Award is given in the light

h< Whether the action of management of State Bank

£h r Lyak Singh, Peon-cum-Frash, is just, lair and

On perusal of the statement of claim and written

L That on the 26th November, 1982, at about 5.1Q p r m. 
you 7 unauthorised!}'took away gunny bags used by 
the branch for packing of stationery. Shri Ravi Chand,

from the branch- You thus, did act prejudicial to the 
interest of the bank . 

2. Thai you do not wear the offic ia I uniform supplied to 
you. You were advised to the Manager vide office 
order No, 12 dated 7-3-83 to wear the uniform but you 
did not comply with his lawful orders. Vide office 
order No, \ 7 dated 17-3-83+ you were again advised 
by the Manager to wear the uniform but you are 
attending ihe office, without, any uniform in truer 
disregard of instructions issued by the Bank. 


3. That, you art alleged to have misplaced two credit 
vouchers dated 19-3-83 for Rs, 740 and 520 relaling

Register of the branch and thus did acr prejudicial to

4 That you were delivered letter No,6Q42 dated the

fr That on 21-9-S3 you abused Sh. Ram Kumar,

oti the ground of violation of principle of natural justice

1. Whether a fair and reasonable enquiry was 
conducted by the enquiry officer in compliance of the 
principle of natural justice. 

2. Whether the enquiry officer was justified to hold 
the chargeNo, 1,5 and 6 well proved against ihe workman, 

3. Whether ihe disciplinary authority and the 
appellate authority have followed the principles of natural 
justice while awarding punishment to the workman and in 
disposal of the appeal against the punishment order.

L As in both the above Industrial Dispute cases a

6. Before appreciating the fads pleaded by the

7. Now the case as set up by both The delinquent

$. The claim ot lIk- workers have been to mesial b;

Lo the employees dnes not <dl for any interference ai lhc

9. In Industrial Impute t’ibe Nn. 21 of 2004, whereas 
workman has adduced oral as well as documentary 
evidence, opposite puily have filed certain documents by 
way of evidence snd ha* viven uny oral evidence nor base 
cross-examined the worker witness. In 22 of 20D-I both 
contesting parties have adduced oral as well as documentary

10. It may be pointed out (hal for appreciating the 
action of the management bn^edon disci pi mar; action the 
evidence led before the Tribunal is being ignored and thev 
will be examined on ibe busL of evidence of parties before 
the enquiry officer. 

11. The Tribunal bus examined carefully the evidence 
adduced by the panics before the Inquiry Officer and have 
also gone through the swat trials available on record. As 
pointed out above the Tribunal is confining its findings to 
the departmental proceedings hold after the order passed 
by the Central Adnunmrative Tribunal directing the 
management to huW euquiis de nuvu.

12. Tribunal has also heard the arguments of the 
contesting parlies at length. 

13. The re presents live for the workmen have 
vehemently argued that in proceed ipgs de-novo held against 
the workers the inquiry officer did not provide the workers 
documents summoned by them nor the material witnesses 
such as Sri Ram Kripal and Sri J K Rai were ever examined 
or cross-examined before the inqiry, therefore, in the 
absence of their evidence charge cannot be held to be 
proved against them. 

M. On the contrary it has been argued by the 
authorized representative for the workers that as both 
delinquent employees have not denied their presence either 
in their pleadings or in their evidence at the place of incident 
on 15-1 0 -JG t nor have stated in their evidence that no such 
incident ever look place as has been mentioned in the 
chargesheet and even in paragraph No. 3 of their statement 
of claim they have categorically admitted the fact that FIR/ 
NCR urn lodged against them at P.S. Rail Bazar, Kanpur, 
on 15* 10-83, there fore, on the basts of dear cut admission 
as above, there was no need for providing any documents 
as called for by them during the course of inquiry as the 
charges automatically stood proved against them. 

J 5. The tribunal is unable to agree wilh the arguments 
advanced by the authorized representative fbr the worker* 
for the reasons that it has come in the pleadings and 
evidence of the parties that some agitation of workers were 
going on regarding certain issues and both of them have 
also admitted their presence at the place agitation. It has 
also been admitted by them that they have come to the 
place of the incident with due permission of his mate and 
also in view of Admitted fact that FIR/NCR were lodged by 
the officer* of the railway against on 15-10-83 at P S, Rail 
Bazar* Kanpur* Taking into consideration the overall aspect 
of the matter it clinches to the mind of the tribunal that how 
these two persons reached at the place of incident leaving 
the place of their duties and mere their presence at the 
place of incident is sufficient to hold that incident took 
place in the manner in which it is stated in the chargesheet 
It can also be easily presumed that when the fact of agitation 
of workers is admitted to both the contesting parties what 
provoked in the mind of the officers of the opposite parties 
to have lodged FIR against both the employees leaving 
several others who are also alleged to be the participants 
of the agitation. Therefore the arguments advanced by the 
representative for the opposite party appears on sound 
footings and it is very difficult to disbelieve the case of the 
opposite party under the facts and circumstances of the 
case. The arguments advanced by the representative of 
the worker is wholly devoid of merit in view of finding of 
the Central Administrative Tribunal, Allahabad, in its order 
dated 12-04-02, that there were other evidence available on 
the file to show that incident had taken place and that the 


applicants had abused, insulted and slapped the said PW1 
and there Is no requirement to rely on the statements made 
by these offices as even without their statements iht charge 
would still be proved against the applicants. This findings 
ofthe Administrative Tribunal, Allahabad, is based on facts 
and therefore, the tribunal has no hesitation to hold that 
for the reasons given above, charge against these 
delinquent employees stands proved even without the help 
of other evidence* therefore, there appears to be no illegality 
when the delinquent employees were not provided with 
the documents called by them from the inquiry officer or 
when the witnesses named in the charge sheet were not 
examined by the opposite party in domestic inquiry. Even 
they have been able to establish the fact as to what 
prejudice had been caused to them ifthe documents called 
for have not been given to them or the management did not 
examined the persons named as witness in the chargesheei. 
It was the legal obligation of the workers to have established 
the fact that they have neither took part in the alleged 
agitation nor have ever abused, insulted or slapped the 
officer of the mil way. Instead they appears to be very much 
inclined to establish evidence against themselves when 
they of their own accord proved that they were very much 
present on the place of incident and that FIR had been 
lodged against them by the o fficer of the opposite party on 
15-10-83. Therefore* in view of their own admission, there 
remains hardly any need for the opposite party to establish 
the charges as the charges stands proved from the 
pleadings and evidence of the workers* 

16- No other points have been pressed by the 
representatives for the contesting parties. Accordingly it 
is held that the charges against both the delinquent 
employees stands tolly established and there is no illegality 
in the action of the opposite party when they passed 
removal order against both of them by way of punishment. 

17. For the reasons explained above, it is held that 
the action of the management in removing both the 
delinquent employee from service vide order dated 9-1 -03 
passed by the disciplinary authority and upheld by the 
appellate authority vide order dated 20-6-03, is neither illegal 
nor unjustified. The result is that the reference is answered 
in negative against the workers holding that they are not 
entitled for any relief as claimed by them in their statement 
of claim. And accordingly their claim stands rejected.

the following dispute for adjudication to this tribunal:— 

KYA SAHAYAK M AND A L AB HIYA N TA UTTA R

4. The undisputed facts of the case in both the cases 
are that the workmen were regular and permanent employee 
of the opposite party arid they were giver the status of at 
the post of gang man by the opposite party. Both of them 
were served with major penalty charge sheet dated 
28-10-83, for manhandling with Gang Mate Sri Ram Kjipal 
misbehaving with him and on the ground that he left the 
place of duty unauthorized manner without permission 
which act was of serious nature. After holding departmental 
enquiry against both of them the Enquiry Officer submitted 
his report dated 2-11-88 and acting upon the said inquiry 
report the disciplinary authority by means of its order dated 
30-12-1993 removed the sendees of both (he delinquent 
employee from the service of railway. Both delinquent 
employees preferred separate appeals before the appellate 
authority and the appellate authority after considering the 
nature of misconduct vide its order dated 26-04-1994 
rejected the appeal of the employees. Thereafter both of 
them approached before the Central Administrative 
Tribunal, Allahabad, by ftlingO.A.No. 1043 of 1994 and O, 
A. No. 1043 of 1994, whereby the order of the disciplinary 
authority and the appellate authority were challenged by 
them. Administrative Tribunal allowed the applicant Vide 
its order dated 12-04-03 by setting aside the impugned 
orders with direction that the petitioner shall remain under 
deemed suspension with further direction that de novo 
inquiry from the stage of 06-1J -87 be held in the matter and 
the opposite party are free to proceed against the delinquent 
employee relying on the settled law that where there is 
procedural irregularity In holding of enquiry, the enquiry 
report and consequential orders may be quashed and set 
aside but the matter should be remanded back to the 
authorities to start inquiry from the stage where (he said 
irregularity has taken place. The order 12-04-02 is quite 
indicative of the fact that the perusal of the enquiry report 
shows that apart from the evidence of three officers as 
mentioned above, there were other evidence available on 
the file to show that the incident had taken place and that 
the applicants had abused, insulted and slapped the said 
P. W.l and there is no requirement to rely on the statement 
made by these officers as even without their statements 
the charge would still be proved against the applicants. 

5. In view of it tribunal has no hes i tat ion in examining 
- <hc enquiry conducted by the department from the stage 
of 06-1J -8? under the orders of the Industrial Tribunal. 

6 Before appreciating the facts pleaded by the 
contesting patties and evidence adduced by the parties 
both the delinquent employee have not denied their 
presence at the pi ace of incident on 15-10-83 and also it is 
not in dispute that F.I.R was lodged against both the 
employees hy the officers of the Railway regarding the 
incident of manhandling, abusing and slapping the officers 
of the opposite parties by these delinquent employees. It 
is also not in dispute that both of them have left the place

7, Now the ca-ie us set up by both the delinquent

It is further pleaded hy [hem that ihe star wimesses Mich

Lbey were'pro JuceO for their cross-examination at the luoids

Enquiry by the enquiry officer. Thus the inquiry eon dueled 
in their case is against the rules and nut urn! justice, mid

9. In Industrial Diipuiv Cose 21 of 2004, wliereo* 
workman has adduced oral lis ^ve)l as documentary 
evidence, opposite party luve filed certain ducoments by 
way of evidence and ha* given any oral evidence; nor haw

10. I[ may be pointed oui lh;it Ibr appreciating The

11. The tribunal h;i' examined carefully the evidence

13. The representative for the workmen have 
vehemently argued Thai in proceedings tte novo held £i gal list 
e he workers the in q u i iy o IH c er d id n ut provi de th e w ork c i s 
documents summoned by them noi die material witness 
^uch Sri Ham Kripal ami Sri J K Rni were ever examined 
or cros 5 -examined before the iitquiiy, therefore, sii the 
absence of their evidence charge cannot be held to he 
proved again.st them. 

14. On the cotuiiLiy u has been argued b> the 
authorized vepresenLuive for ihe workers that as both 
ddinquenl employee a liavj nut denied their presence either 
i a their plead in gs or i n the: r e v idc nec at the p I a cc of i ne i dcu t 
on 15-10-83, nor have q.ued m their evidence that no such 
incident ever took place n>i luts hcen men(ionod in the 
charge shed and even hi puny raph no. 3 of their state meitL 
of claim they have emev^r-. rally admitted die factthai E : ]IL : 
SCR was lodged ogaiuq them ut P.S. Rail Hazar, tan pur, 
on ] 5 -10-8 3, tiier eio re, o n l h c ha s i s o f c lear c Lit ad m i ion 
as above, there was no need ibr providing any documents 
as called for by ti unit LJumg the course of inquiry »s the 
charges automatically ^-.lod proved against them. 

Id. 1 'he tribiu io I k l n mb I e U m grec w Lih the argLinie rtls 
advanced by the authori/.vti representative tbi' the workers 
for ihe reasons that ii has come in the pleadings and 
evidence of ihe parties ih.ai some agiLaduii of workers were 
goinc on regarding cti-tum issue a and both of ihe m hove 
also admitted ther piv^sicc at the place agiiution. It has 
li I so been admitted by I'acni LhaL they have come to the 








[Mmn—gqg 3(jj>] 


IB, aooa/gnfi^ 26, 1930 


5 ft 53 


place of the incident with due permission of his mate and 
also in view of admitted feet that FIR/NCR were lodged by 
the officers of die railway against them on 13-10-83 at P.S. 
Rail Bazar, Kanpur, Taking into consideration die overall 
aspect of the matter it clinches to the mind of the tribunal 
that how these two persons reached at the place of incident 
leaving the place of their duties and mere their presence at 
the place of incident is sufficient to hold that incident took 
place in the manner in which it i$ stated in the charge sheet. 
It can also be easily presumed that when (he fact of agitation 
of workers is admitted to both the contesting parties what 
provoked in the mind of the officers of the opposite parties 
to have lodged FIR against both the employees leaving 
several others who are also alleged to be die partic i pant 
of the agitation. Therefore die arguments advanced by the 
representative for the opposite party appears on sound 
footings and it is very difficult to disbelieve the case of the 
opposite party under the facts and circumstances of the 
case. The arguments advanced by the representative of 
the worker is wholly devoid of merit in view of finding, of 
the Cetral Administrative Tribunal, Allahabad, in its order 
dated ] 2-04*02, that there were other evidence available on 
the file to show that incident had taken place and that the 
applicants had abused, insulted and slapped the said PWl 
and there is no requirement to rely on (he statements made 
by these offices as even without their statements the charge 
would still be proved against the applicants. This findings 
of the Administrative Tribunal, Allahabad, is based on foots 
and therefore, the tribunal has no hesitation to hold that 
for the reasons given above, charge against these 
delinquent employees stands proved even without the help 
of other evidence, therefore, there appears (o be no 
illegality when the delinquent employees were not provided 
with the documents called by them from the inquiry officer 
or when the witnesses named in the charge sheet were not 
examined by the opposite party in domestic inquiry. Even 
they have been able to establish the fact as to what 
prejudice had been caused to them if the documents called 
for have not been given to them or the management did not 
examined the persons named as witness in the charge sheet 
It was die legal obligation ofthe workers to have established 
the fact that they have neither took part in the alleged 
agitation nor have ever abused, insulted or slapped the 
officer oftherailway. Instead they appears to be very much 
inclined to establish evidence against themselves when 
they of their own accord proved that they were very much 
present on die place of incident and that FIR had been 
lodged against them by the officer ofthe opposite party on 
15-10-83. Therefore, in view of their own admission, there 
remains hardly any need for the opposite party to establish 
the charges as the charges stands proved from the 
pleadings and evidence of the workers. 

16. No other points have been pressed by the 
representatives for the contesting parties. Accordingly 
it is held that the charges against both the delinquent 
employees stands fblty established and there is no 
illegality in the action of the opposite party when they 


passed removal order against both of them by way of 
punishment. 

17, For the reasons explained above, it is held that 
the action of the management in removing both the 
delinquent employees from service vide order dated 9-1 -03 
passed by the disciplinary authority and upheld by the 
appellate authority vide order dated20-6-03, is neither illegal 
nor unjustified. The result is that the reference b answered 
in negative against the workers bolding that they are not 
entitled for any relief ns claimed by them in their statement 
of claim. And accordingly tiieir claim stands rejected.

of law to the case of applicant it is quite evident that the

rejected and is hereby rejected,
